I-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})=\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})(\delta_{1}^{2}L_{1}+\delta_{2}^{2}L_{2})^{r}
.
Since
|{\bm{\lambda}}|^{2r}=(\lambda_{1}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2})^{r}
is a radial function
from the definition of
\varphi
it follows that
\omega
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
4.21
.
Hence

(5.16)

{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\leq c_{\mathbf{n}}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\mathbf{n}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),\quad{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in X.

From the representation

(\delta_{1}^{2}L_{1}+\delta_{2}^{2}L_{2})^{r}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\binom{r}{\ell}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}L_{1}^{\ell}L_{2}^{r-\ell}

and assuming
0\leq\nu_{i}\leq m
,
we get

(5.17)

\displaystyle L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\big(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi\big)({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle=L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})(\delta_{1}^{2}L_{1}+\delta_{2}^{2}L_{2})^{r}\phi({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\binom{r}{\ell}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\omega(\delta\sqrt{L})L^{(\nu_{1}+\ell,\nu_{2}+r-\ell)}\phi({\mathbf{x}}).

Pick
N\geq(n_{1}-d_{1}/2)\vee(n_{2}-d_{2}/2)
and assume
{\bm{0}}<{\bm{\delta}}\leq{\bm{1}}
,
{\bm{1}}:=(1,1)
.
Then using (
5.16
)–(
5.17
) we obtain

\displaystyle|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\big(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi\big)({\mathbf{x}})|\leq c\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\mathbf{n}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|L^{(\nu_{1}+\ell,\nu_{2}+r-\ell)}\phi({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\mathcal{P}_{m+r,N}(\phi)\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\mathbf{n}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\mathcal{P}_{m+r,N}(\phi)\int_{X}\frac{1}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\mathcal{P}_{m+r,N}(\phi){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\mathbf{n}}-{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}),

where for the third inequality we used (
3.14
)
and for the last inequality we used (
3.22
) and that
n_{i}-d_{i}/2>2d_{i}
.
Finally, putting all together we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi)

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\mathcal{P}_{m+r,N}(\phi)\sup_{{\mathbf{x}}\in X}\prod_{i=1,2}\frac{\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{k}}{\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{n_{i}-d_{i}/2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m+r,N}(\phi)\sum_{\ell=0}^{r}\delta_{1}^{2\ell}\delta_{2}^{2(r-\ell)}\rightarrow 0\;\text{ as }\;\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\rightarrow 0,

where we used that
n_{i}\geq k+d_{i}/2
.
The proof of (
5.15
) is complete.
∎

We next derive a Calderón type reproducing formula in the setting of this article.
(Compare with the result from Lemma
4.15
.)
It relies on two pairs of functions
\varphi^{i}_{0},\varphi^{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
i=1,2
, with the properties:

(5.18)

\displaystyle(i)\;\;\hbox{$\varphi^{i}_{0},\varphi^{i}$ are real-valued and even,}

\displaystyle(ii)\ \operatorname{supp}\varphi^{i}_{0}\subset[-2,2],\ \operatorname{supp}\varphi^{i}\subset[2^{-1},2]\setminus[-1/2,1/2],

and

(5.19)

\varphi^{i}_{0}(t)+\sum_{n\geq 1}\varphi^{i}(2^{-n}t)=1,\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

It is easy to construct functions
\varphi^{i}_{0},\varphi^{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
i=1,2
, with the above properties.
Indeed, let
\varphi_{0}^{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
(
i=1,2
) be an even real-valued function satisfying the conditions:
(a)
\varphi_{0}^{i}(t)=1
,
t\in[-1,1]
,
(b)
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{0}^{i}\subset[-2,2]
,
(c)
0\leq\varphi_{0}^{i}\leq 1
,
and define
\varphi^{i}(t):=\varphi^{i}_{0}(t)-\varphi_{0}^{i}(2t)
.
Then it follows immediately that

\varphi^{i}_{0},\varphi^{i}
satisfy conditions (
5.18
)–(
5.19
).

Using the standard notation
\varphi^{i}_{n}(t):=\varphi^{i}(2^{-n}t),n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
for any
{\mathbf{j}}=(j_{1},j_{2})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}_{0}
we set
\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}:=\varphi^{1}_{j_{1}}\otimes\varphi^{2}_{j_{2}}
.
Then from (
5.19
) it readily follows that

(5.20)

\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}({\bm{\lambda}})=1,\quad\forall{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\varphi^{1}_{0},\varphi^{2}_{0},\varphi^{1},\varphi^{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be functions
satisfying
(\ref{phi-phi0})
–
(\ref{1dCalderon})

and let
\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}
be as above.
Then, for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

(5.21)

\phi=\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\;\mathcal{S}

and, therefore, for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

(5.22)

f=\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})f\quad\mbox{ in }\;\mathcal{S}^{\prime}.

Proof.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}
(resp.
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
).
We set
\theta^{i}(t):=\varphi^{i}_{0}(t)+\varphi^{i}(2^{-1}t)
,
i=1,2
,
and observe that (
5.18
)–(
5.19
)
imply
\operatorname{supp}\theta^{i}\subset[-4,4]
and
\theta^{i}(t)=1
for
t\in[-2,2]
.
Moreover from the definition of
\theta^{i}
and (
5.19
) we have

\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+1}\varphi^{i}_{n}(t)=\theta^{i}\big(2^{-\ell}t\big),\quad\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

Set
\theta:=\theta^{1}\otimes\theta^{2}
. Then it is easily seen that for any
{\mathbf{k}}:=(k_{1},k_{2})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}
,

\sum_{j_{1}=0}^{k_{1}+1}\sum_{j_{2}=0}^{k_{2}+1}\varphi_{{\mathbf{j}}}({\bm{\lambda}})=(\theta^{1}\otimes\theta^{2})(b_{1}^{-k_{1}}\lambda_{1},b_{2}^{-k_{2}}\lambda_{2})=\theta\big(b^{-{\mathbf{k}}}{\bm{\lambda}}\big).

Finally, applying Theorem
5.8
to
\theta
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})f

\displaystyle=\lim_{k_{1},k_{2}\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{j_{1}=0}^{k_{1}+1}\sum_{j_{2}=0}^{k_{2}+1}\big(\varphi^{1}_{j_{1}}(\sqrt{L_{1}})\otimes\varphi^{2}_{j_{2}}(\sqrt{L_{2}})\big)(f)

\displaystyle=\lim_{k_{1},k_{2}\rightarrow\infty}\theta(b^{-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})f=f,

which completes the proof.
∎

Our next step is to establish our principle convergence result.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be a real-valued function satisfying the conditions:

\varphi({\bm{0}})=1

and

\varphi(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\varphi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
for
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Then for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

(5.23)

\phi=\lim_{{\bm{\delta}}\to{\bm{0}}}\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\;\mathcal{S}

and, therefore, for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

(5.24)

f=\lim_{{\bm{\delta}}\to{\bm{0}}}\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})f\quad\mbox{ in }\;\mathcal{S}^{\prime}.

Furthermore, (
5.24
) holds in
L^{p}
for any
f\in L^{p}
,
1\leq p\leq\infty
,
where
L^{\infty}={\rm UCB}
is the space of all uniformly continuous and bounded functions on
X
.

For the proof of this theorem we need the following

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\bm{0}}
,

k\geq(\sigma_{1}+d_{1})\vee(\sigma_{2}+d_{2})
, and

m>\frac{d_{1}+\alpha_{1}}{2}\vee\frac{d_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{2}

with
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
from
(\ref{lip})
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that
for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
and
{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in X

(5.25)

|\phi({\mathbf{x}})-\phi({\mathbf{y}})|\leq c\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)\big[{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})+{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})].

Proof.

Let
\varphi^{1}_{0},\varphi^{2}_{0},\varphi^{1},\varphi^{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be functions
satisfying
(\ref{phi-phi0})
–
(\ref{1dCalderon})

and
let
\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}:=\varphi^{1}_{j_{1}}\otimes\varphi^{2}_{j_{2}}

be just as in Corollary
5.9
.
Then by Corollary
5.9

(5.26)

\phi=\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\;\mathcal{S}.

We write

(5.27)

\displaystyle\phi({\mathbf{x}})-\phi({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}}\big(\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})-\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{y}})\big)=:\sum_{k=1}^{4}\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{k}}U_{{\mathbf{j}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),

where
\mathcal{J}_{1}:={\mathbb{N}}^{2}
,
\mathcal{J}_{2}:=\{(0,0)\}
,
\mathcal{J}_{3}:={\mathbb{N}}\times\{0\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{4}:=\{0\}\times{\mathbb{N}}
.

We next estimate
\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}}|U_{{\mathbf{j}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|
.
Let
{\mathbf{m}}:=(m,m)

and set

\omega(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=|\lambda_{1}|^{-2m}|\lambda_{2}|^{-2m}\varphi^{1}(\lambda_{1})\varphi^{2}(\lambda_{2}).

Clearly,
\omega(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\omega(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
and
\operatorname{supp}\omega\subset[-2,2]\setminus[-1,1]
.
For
{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}={\mathbb{N}}^{2}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

we have

\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi=2^{-2m(j_{1}+j_{2})}\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})L^{\mathbf{m}}\phi

and, therefore,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})-

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{y}})

(5.28)

\displaystyle=2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}\int_{X}\big({\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})-{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})\big)L^{{\mathbf{m}}}\phi({\mathbf{z}})d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

By Theorem
4.18
it readily follows that

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})|

\displaystyle\leq c_{{\bm{\sigma}}}{\mathfrak{D}}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\sigma}}+3{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})\leq cV({\mathbf{x}},2^{-{\mathbf{j}}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq c2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}}),

in light of (
3.14
) and (
3.12
).
Moreover, if
\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq 2^{-j_{i}}
,
i=1,2
, then

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})-

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq c\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}(2^{j_{i}}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}}),

Note also that, since
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
,

(5.31)

|L^{{\mathbf{m}}}\phi({\mathbf{z}})|\leq\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}).

Combining (
5.2
), (
5.30
) and (
5.31
) we conclude that
if
\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq 2^{-j_{i}}
,
i=1,2
, then

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle-\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{y}})|\leq c2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}(2^{j_{i}}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)

(5.32)

\displaystyle\times V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\bm{\alpha}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}),

where we used (
3.20
).

Assume
\rho_{\iota}(x_{\iota},y_{\iota})>2^{-j_{\iota}}
for some
\iota\in\{1,2\}
.
Then by (
5.29
), (
5.31
) and (
3.20
) we obtain, for
{\mathbf{x}}\in X
,

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})|\leq 2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}\int_{X}|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})||L^{{\mathbf{m}}}\phi({\mathbf{z}})|d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)(2^{\iota}\rho_{\iota}(x_{\iota},y_{\iota}))^{\alpha_{\iota}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}+{\mathbf{d}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\bm{\alpha}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}),

implying

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})-\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{y}})|\leq|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{x}})|+|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}(\sqrt{L})\phi({\mathbf{y}})|

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-2m|{\mathbf{j}}|}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\mathbf{d}}}2^{{\mathbf{j}}\cdot{\bm{\alpha}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)\big[{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})+{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})\big].

Summing up (
5.2
) and the above inequalities over
{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}={\mathbb{N}}^{2}
,
using the assumption that

m>\frac{d_{1}+\alpha_{1}}{2}\vee\frac{d_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{2}
,
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{1}}

\displaystyle|U_{{\mathbf{j}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|

(5.33)

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(\phi)\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)\big[{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})+{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})\big].

The estimation of the sums
\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in\mathcal{J}_{i}}|U_{{\mathbf{j}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|
,
i=2,3,4
,
is similar (simpler) and we omit it.
The desired estimate (
5.25
) follows from these estimates, (
5.27
), and (
5.2
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

We will prove (
5.23
). Then (
5.24
) follows by duality.
We need to show that for any
n,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
and
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

(5.34)

\lim_{{\bm{\delta}}\to{\bm{0}}}\mathcal{P}_{n,\ell}(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi)=0.

By Theorem
4.21
it follows that for any
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\bm{0}}
there exists a constant
c_{{\bm{\sigma}}}>0
such that

(5.35)

|{\mathscr{K}}_{\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|\leq c_{\bm{\sigma}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

and

(5.36)

\int_{X}{\mathscr{K}}_{\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})=\varphi({\bm{0}})=1.

Fix
n,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
and let
{\bm{\ell}}:=(\ell,\ell)
and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}
be so that
0\leq\nu_{i}\leq n
,
i=1,2
.
We choose
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\mathbf{d}}+{\bm{\ell}}+{\bm{\alpha}}
.
Also, let
m,k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
be just as in Lemma
5.11
,
i.e.
m>\frac{d_{1}+\alpha_{1}}{2}\vee\frac{d_{2}+\alpha_{2}}{2}
and
k\geq(\sigma_{1}+d_{1})\vee(\sigma_{2}+d_{2})
.
To prove (
5.34
) we have to deal with

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}

\displaystyle({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi)({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{X}|{\mathscr{K}}_{\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})||L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi({\mathbf{x}})-L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

(5.37)

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{X}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi({\mathbf{x}})-L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\dots+c{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{X\setminus Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\dots

\displaystyle=:W_{1}({\mathbf{x}})+W_{2}({\mathbf{x}}),

where we used (
5.35
) and (
5.36
).
Recall that
Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}}):=B_{1}(x_{1},1)\times B_{2}(x_{2},1)
, see (
3.8
).

We focus first on the estimation of
W_{1}({\mathbf{x}})
. As
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
we have
L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi\in\mathcal{S}
,
and in light of (
5.1
)

\mathcal{P}_{m,k}(L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi)\leq\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)
.
We use this and Lemma
5.11
to obtain

\displaystyle W_{1}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\int_{Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}\Big)\big[{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})+{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})]\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

(5.38)

\displaystyle=c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)\Big(\int_{Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})\cdots+\int_{Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})\cdots\Big)

\displaystyle=:c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)(J_{1}({\mathbf{x}})+J_{2}({\mathbf{x}})).

To estimate
J_{1}({\mathbf{x}})
we note first that

\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}<\delta_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\alpha_{i}}
,

i=1,2
,
and hence

(5.39)

\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}<\frac{\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}}{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}.

From the choice of
{\bm{\sigma}}
it follows that
{\bm{\sigma}}\geq{\bm{\ell}}
and
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\mathbf{d}}+{\bm{\alpha}}
.
Then using these, (
5.39
), and (
3.19
)) we get

(5.40)

\displaystyle J_{1}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq c(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}})V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\leq c(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

We next estimate
J_{2}({\mathbf{x}})
. We may assume
{\bm{\delta}}<{\bm{1}}
.
The obvious inequalities

1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\leq(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))(1+\rho_{i}(y_{i},x_{0i}))
,
i=1,2
,
implies that

{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\leq{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}.

From this and (
5.39
) it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{(\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})}

(5.41)

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\leq(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\ell}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\leq(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}){\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\ell}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),

where we used that
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\bm{\ell}}
.
We use (
5.2
) and (
3.19
) and the fact that
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\mathbf{d}}+{\bm{\ell}}+{\bm{\alpha}}
to derive

J_{2}({\mathbf{x}})\leq c(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}})V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\leq c(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

From this, (
5.2
), and (
5.40
) it follows that

(5.42)

W_{1}(x)\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

We next estimate
W_{2}({\mathbf{x}})
. We split it into two:

\displaystyle W_{2}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{X\setminus Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}(\phi)({\mathbf{x}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

(5.43)

\displaystyle+c{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}\int_{X\setminus Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}(\phi)({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=:W_{21}({\mathbf{x}})+W_{22}({\mathbf{x}}).

Note that because
0\leq\nu_{i}\leq n
and
k>\sigma_{1}\vee\sigma_{2}
we have

(5.44)

|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\phi({\mathbf{z}})|\leq\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0}),\quad\forall{\mathbf{z}}\in X,

and using that
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\bm{\ell}}
we obtain

(5.45)

W_{21}({\mathbf{x}})\leq\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)\int_{X\setminus Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

Clearly, for any
{\mathbf{y}}\in X\setminus Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{1}})
, there exists
\iota\in\{1,2\}
such that
\rho_{\iota}(x_{\iota},y_{\iota})>1

and hence

(5.46)

1<\delta_{\iota}^{\alpha_{\iota}}(1+\delta_{\iota}^{-1}\rho_{\iota}(x_{\iota},y_{\iota}))^{\alpha_{\iota}}<\frac{\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}}{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}.

Combining (
5.45
), (
5.46
) and using (
3.19
) and the fact that
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\mathbf{d}}+{\bm{\alpha}}
we get

(5.47)

\displaystyle W_{21}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}})\int_{X}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

Similarly

\displaystyle W_{22}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}})\int_{X}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})}{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{1}},{\mathbf{m}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

(5.48)

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}})\int_{X}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}-{\bm{\ell}}-{\bm{\alpha}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}),

where we used (
3.19
) and the fact that
{\bm{\sigma}}>{\mathbf{d}}+{\bm{\ell}}+{\bm{\alpha}}
.
Combining (
5.2
), (
5.47
), and (
5.2
) we get

(5.49)

W_{2}(x)\leq c\mathcal{P}_{n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

Finally, (
5.42
) and (
5.49
) yield

{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\ell}}}^{*}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{x}}_{0})^{-1}|L^{{\bm{\nu}}}(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi)({\mathbf{x}})|\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}),

and hence

\mathcal{P}_{n,\ell}\big(\phi-\varphi({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})\phi\big)\leq c\mathcal{P}_{m+n,k}(\phi)(\delta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}+\delta_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}).

In turn this yields (
5.34
).

The convergence in (
5.24
) in
L^{p}
for
f\in L^{p}
follows by a standard argument using (
5.35
) and (
5.36
).
∎

6.
Spectral spaces

In this section we deal with spectral spaces,
which can be viewed as a generalization of the band limited functions in the classical setting on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Our main goal here is to establish Peetre and Nikolski type inequalities that will be used in what follows.

Definition 6.1
.

The spectral space
\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
,
{\mathbf{t}}=(t_{1},t_{2})\in(0,\infty)^{2}
, is defined by

(6.1)

\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}:=\{g\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}:\theta(\sqrt{L})g=g,\text{ for all }\theta\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{t}}\},\quad\mathcal{S}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(L_{1},L_{2}),

where

\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{t}}:=\{\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}):\theta(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\theta(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\text{ and }\theta\equiv 1\text{ on }[0,t_{1}]\times[0,t_{2}]\}

with
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
being the set of all compactly supported
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
functions on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

The above definition and Proposition
5.7
imply that each
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}

is a slowly growing continuous function.

Proposition 6.2
.

Each
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
,
{\mathbf{t}}\in(0,\infty)^{2}
, is a slowly growing continuous function in the sense that
there exist constants
N,c>0

(
depending on
g
)
such that

(6.2)

\displaystyle|g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq cV({\mathbf{x}}_{0},1)^{-1}\prod_{i=1,2}(t_{i}^{N}+t_{i}^{-N})\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{N}\quad\hbox{and}

(6.3)

\displaystyle|g({\mathbf{x}})-g({\mathbf{x}}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq cV({\mathbf{x}}_{0},1)^{-1}\prod_{i=1,2}(t_{i}^{N}+t_{i}^{-N})\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{N}\sum_{i=1,2}\rho_{i}(x_{i},x^{\prime}_{i})^{\alpha_{i}}

if
\rho_{i}(x_{i},x^{\prime}_{i})\leq t_{i}^{-1}
,
i=1,2
.
Here
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
are from (
2.4
).

The following simple claim will be needed:

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be real-valued,

\varphi(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\varphi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
,
and
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset[-t_{1},t_{1}]\times[-t_{2},t_{2}]
,
t_{1},t_{2}>0
.
Then for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(L_{1},L_{2})
we have

\varphi(\sqrt{L})f\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
.

Proof.

Let
\theta
be a function as in Definition
6.1
.
Then
\theta({\bm{\lambda}})\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})=\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})
and in light of Proposition
5.5

for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
we have

\theta(\sqrt{L})\varphi(\sqrt{L})f=\varphi(\sqrt{L})f,

which completes the proof.
∎

6.1.
Peetre type maximal inequality

For reader’s convenience we recall the following compact notation from (
3.15
);
it will be used in what follows:

(6.4)

V({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}:=V_{1}(x_{1},t_{1}^{-1})^{\gamma_{1}}V_{2}(x_{2},t_{2}^{-1})^{\gamma_{2}},\quad{\mathbf{t}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+},{\bm{\gamma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
{\bm{\gamma}},{\bm{\tau}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
{\bm{\tau}}>2{\mathbf{d}}
,
r>0
, and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that
for any
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
with
{\mathbf{t}}\in[1,\infty)^{2}
we have

(6.5)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{t}}^{-2{\bm{\nu}}}\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+t_{i}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+t_{i}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}\leq c\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}}),\;\;{\mathbf{x}}\in X.

Proof.

We first establish the left-hand side inequality in (
6.5
).

Let
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
,
{\mathbf{t}}\in[1,\infty)^{2}
, and set
{\bm{\delta}}:={\mathbf{t}}^{-1}=(t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}^{-1})
.
Let
\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\theta(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\theta(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})

and
\theta({\bm{\lambda}})=1
for
{\bm{\lambda}}\in[0,1]^{2}
.
Then by (
6.1
)
g=\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g
.
Let
\omega(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=\lambda_{1}^{2\nu_{1}}\lambda_{2}^{2\nu_{2}}\theta(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
.
Clearly,

\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})={\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})

and hence

(6.6)

L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{y}})=L^{\bm{\nu}}\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{y}})={\bm{\delta}}^{-2{\bm{\nu}}}\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{y}})={\bm{\delta}}^{-2{\bm{\nu}}}\int_{X}{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})g({\mathbf{z}})d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

We use Corollary
4.22
and (
3.14
) to conclude that
for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in(0,\infty)^{2}

(6.7)

|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|\leq c_{\bm{\sigma}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}}).

Therefore,

(6.8)

{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}|L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{y}})|\leq cV({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|g({\mathbf{z}})|d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

We know from (
3.15
) that

(6.9)

V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}\leq cV({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}.

and obviously

(6.10)

1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\leq(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i})),\quad i=1,2.

We use these inequalities and (
6.8
) with
\sigma_{i}:=\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|+d_{i}+1

to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|+d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq cV({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}},

which implies the left-hand side inequality in (
6.5
).
Above for the last inequality we used (
3.19
).

To prove the right-hand side inequality in (
6.5
) we consider three cases.

Case 1:

1<r<\infty
.
With the notation from above it follows from Corollary
4.22
and (
3.14
) that
for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in(0,\infty)^{2}

(6.11)

|{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|\leq c_{\bm{\sigma}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

and hence

(6.12)

|g({\mathbf{y}})|=|\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})({\mathbf{y}})|\leq cV({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|g({\mathbf{z}})|d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

Choose
\sigma_{i}:=\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|+(d_{i}+1)/r^{\prime}
,
i=1,2
, where
1/r+1/r^{\prime}=1
.
Then just as above we use (
6.9
) and (
6.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|+(d_{i}+1)/r^{\prime}}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{(d_{i}+1)/r^{\prime}}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

Applying now Hölder’s inequality we get

\displaystyle\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\Big(\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})\Big)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.95122pt\times\Big(\int_{X}\frac{1}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(y_{i},z_{i})\big)^{d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})\Big)^{1/r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}}),

where for the last inequality we used Lemma
4.23
and (
3.19
).
The right-hand side inequality in (
6.5
) follows from above.

Case 2:

r=1
.
The proof in this case is similar to the above proof but is easier; we omit it.

Case 3:

0<r<1
.
To prove the right-hand side inequality in (
6.5
) in this case we first show that
it is valid with a constant
c=c(g)
depending on
g
.

The function
\theta({\bm{\lambda}})
from above obeys the assumptions of Proposition
5.7

and hence there exist constants
N,c>0
, depending on
g
, such that

(6.13)

\displaystyle|g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle=|\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq c(g)V({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\prod_{i=1,2}(\delta_{i}^{N}+\delta_{i}^{-N})\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{N}.

We may assume
N\geq(\tau_{1}\vee\tau_{2})/r
.
On the other hand, from (
3.17
) we obtain

(6.14)

\displaystyle V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq cV({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}

\displaystyle\leq cV({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1})^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}.

This coupled with (
6.13
) implies

(6.15)

V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{x}})|\leq c(g,{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{\tilde{N}},

where
\tilde{N}:=N+|\gamma_{1}|d_{1}+|\gamma_{2}|d_{2}
.
In turn, (
6.15
) with
{\mathbf{x}}
replaced by
{\mathbf{z}}
and the fact that
\delta_{i}=t_{i}^{-1}\leq 1
lead to the estimates

\displaystyle\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}}\leq\frac{c\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(z_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}},

implying

(6.16)

\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}}\leq c(g,{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{\tilde{N}}.

Choose
\sigma_{i}:=\tilde{N}+|\gamma_{1}|d_{1}+|\gamma_{2}|d_{2}
and set
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}:=\tilde{N}
,
i=1,2
.
As before using (
6.11
), (
6.9
), and (
6.10
) we obtain

\displaystyle V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|

\displaystyle=V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}\Big|\int_{X}{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})g({\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{y}})|g({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},\tilde{{\bm{\sigma}}}}({\mathbf{z}},{\mathbf{y}})|g({\mathbf{y}})|d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(z_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c\Big(\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}\Big)^{1-r}

\displaystyle\hskip 115.63243pt\times\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}r}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

We now take
\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}
above and use the fact that by (
6.16
)

\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}\leq c(g,{\bm{\delta}},{\mathbf{x}})<\infty,

which allows us to divide and obtain

(6.17)

\Big(\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}\Big)^{r}\\
\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}r}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

Taking
{\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{x}}
on the left we get

[V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{x}})|]^{r}\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}r}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

Using this estimate with
{\mathbf{z}}
in the place of
{\mathbf{x}}
and the fact that
\tilde{N}r\geq\tau_{1}\vee\tau_{2}

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{[V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|]^{r}}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tau_{i}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tau_{i}}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(z_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{r}}{V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}-d_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}),

where for the first inequality we used (
6.10
) and for the last (
3.17
).
Applying Lemma
4.23
using that
\tau_{i}>2d_{i}

we obtain

(6.18)

\frac{[V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|]^{r}}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tau_{i}}}\leq c(g)\big[\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}})\big]^{r},

which implies the right-hand side inequality in (
6.5
).
Here the constant depends on
g
because
N
depends on
g
.

We are now prepared to complete the proof of the right-hand inequality in (
6.5
).
Recall the notation (see (
3.8
))

Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}}):=B_{1}(x_{1},\delta_{1})\times B_{2}(x_{2},\delta_{2})\quad\hbox{and}\quad{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}):=\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i}))^{-\sigma_{i}}

and observe that
\mu(Q({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}}))=V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
, see (
3.9
).

Using the operator
\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})
from above we have

(6.19)

g({\mathbf{x}})=\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{x}})=\int_{M}{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})g({\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

Let
{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}}\in X
and assume
{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})
.
By Theorem
4.18
and (
3.14
) it follows that for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in(0,\infty)^{2}

there exist a constant
c>0
such that

(6.20)

\big|{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})-{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{z}})\big|\leq cV({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\sum_{i=1,2}\big(\rho_{i}(y_{i},u_{i})/\delta_{i}\big)^{\alpha_{i}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}}).

Fix
0<\varepsilon<1
.
Then

|g({\mathbf{y}})|\leq\inf_{u\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{u}})|+\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{y}})-g({\mathbf{u}})|.

Denote

g^{*}({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{\tau_{i}/r}}=\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}/r}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|g({\mathbf{y}})|.

Then

\displaystyle g^{*}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{\delta,{\bm{\tau}}/r}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\inf_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{u}})|

\displaystyle+\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}/r}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{y}})-g({\mathbf{u}})|

(6.21)

\displaystyle=:\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})+\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}}).

We first estimate
g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})
,
{\mathbf{y}}\in X
.
Clearly,

\inf_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{u}})|\leq\Big(\frac{1}{\mu(Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}}))}\int_{Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{u}})|^{r}d\mu({\mathbf{u}})\Big)^{1/r},

and hence

(6.22)

\displaystyle g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq\mu(Q({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}}))^{{\bm{\gamma}}}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}/r}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big(\frac{1}{\mu(Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}}))}\int_{Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}|g({\mathbf{u}})|^{r}d\mu({\mathbf{u}})\Big)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq c{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}/r}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big(\frac{1}{\mu(Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}}))}\int_{Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})}\mu(Q({\mathbf{u}},{\bm{\delta}}))^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{u}})|\bigr)^{r}d\mu({\mathbf{u}})\Big)^{1/r},

where in the last inequality we used that
\mu(Q({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}}))\sim\mu(Q({\mathbf{u}},{\bm{\delta}}))

since for every
{\mathbf{u}}\in Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}})
,
Q({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\subset Q({\mathbf{u}},2{\bm{\delta}})

and
Q({\mathbf{u}},{\bm{\delta}})\subset Q({\mathbf{y}},2{\bm{\delta}})
.

We introduce the abbreviated notation:

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle:=Q({\mathbf{y}},\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}}),\quad\tilde{Q}:=B_{1}\big(x_{1},\rho_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})+\varepsilon\delta_{1}\big)\times B_{2}\big(x_{2},\rho_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})+\varepsilon\delta_{2}\big),

\displaystyle Q^{*}

\displaystyle:=B_{1}\big(y_{1},2\rho_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})+\varepsilon\delta_{1}\big)\times B_{2}\big(y_{2},2\rho_{2}(x_{2},y_{2})+\varepsilon\delta_{2}\big).

From (
6.22
) taking into account that
Q\subset\tilde{Q}
we get

(6.23)

\displaystyle g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\Big(\frac{\mu(\tilde{Q})}{\mu(Q)}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/r}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu(\tilde{Q})}\int_{Q}\big(V({\mathbf{u}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{u}})|\big)^{r}d\mu({\mathbf{u}})\Big)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq c\Big(\frac{\mu(\tilde{Q})}{\mu(Q)}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/r}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu(\tilde{Q})}\int_{\tilde{Q}}\big(V({\mathbf{u}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{u}})|\big)^{r}d\mu({\mathbf{u}})\Big)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq c\Big(\frac{\mu(\tilde{Q})}{\mu(Q)}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/r}\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}}).

By (
3.12
) and the fact that
0<\varepsilon<1
we get

\mu(\tilde{Q})\leq\mu(Q^{*})\leq c\mu(Q)\prod_{i=1,2}\Big(1+\frac{2\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})}{\varepsilon\delta_{i}}\Big)^{d_{i}}\leq c\varepsilon^{-|{\mathbf{d}}|}\mu(Q){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})^{-1}

and hence

(6.24)

\Big(\frac{\mu(\tilde{Q})}{\mu(Q)}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\tau}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/r}\leq c\varepsilon^{-|{\mathbf{d}}|/r}.

This inequality coupled with (
6.23
) yields

(6.25)

g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})\leq c\varepsilon^{-|{\mathbf{d}}|/r}\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,\delta)^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}}).

We now estimate
g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})
.
Using that
\theta(\delta\sqrt{L})g=g
and (
6.20
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q}|g({\mathbf{y}})-g({\mathbf{u}})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q}\int_{X}|{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta(\delta\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})-{\mathscr{K}}_{\theta(\delta\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{z}})||g({\mathbf{z}})|d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in Q}V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\sum_{i=1,2}\big(\rho_{i}(y_{i},u_{i})/\delta_{i}\big)^{\alpha_{i}}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|g({\mathbf{z}})|d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|g({\mathbf{z}})|d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

Choose
\sigma_{i}:=\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|+d_{i}+1
,
i=1,2
.
From above, the definition of
g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})
, (
6.9
), and (
6.10
) we obtain

\displaystyle g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})}\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}/r}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))^{\sigma_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})}\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))^{d_{i}|\gamma_{i}|}}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}/r}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))^{\sigma_{i}}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})}\int_{X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tau_{i}/r}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))^{\tau_{i}/r+d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tau_{i}/r}}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\times\frac{1}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})}\int_{X}\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))^{d_{i}+1}}d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})g^{*}({\mathbf{x}}).

Thus

g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})\leq\tilde{c}(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})g^{*}({\mathbf{x}}),

where the constant
\tilde{c}
is independent of
\varepsilon
.
We choose
\varepsilon
so that
\tilde{c}(\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}}+\varepsilon^{\alpha_{2}})=1/2
.
Then
\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})\leq g^{*}({\mathbf{x}})/2
which combined with (
6.25
) leads to

\displaystyle g^{*}({\mathbf{x}})\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}g^{*}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})+\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}g^{*}_{2}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c\varepsilon^{-|{\mathbf{d}}|/r}\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}})+g^{*}({\mathbf{x}})/2.

This implies (
6.5
) using that by (
6.18
)
g^{*}({\mathbf{x}})<\infty

if
\mathcal{M}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}})<\infty
.
∎

6.2.
Nikolski type inequality

We will often compare different
L^{p}
-norms of spectral functions using Nikolski type inequalities.

Theorem 6.5
.

(i)

Let
0<p\leq q\leq\infty
,
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}
and
{\bm{\gamma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that
for any
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
with
{\mathbf{t}}\in[1,\infty)^{2}
and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}
we have

(6.26)

\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}L^{{\bm{\nu}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{q}\leq c{\mathbf{t}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}+(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}){\bm{1}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p},\quad{\mathbf{t}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}:=t_{1}^{2\nu_{1}}t_{2}^{2\nu_{2}}.

(ii)

If in addition we assume the non-collapsing condition
(
2.6
)
for the coordinate spaces
X_{1},X_{2}
,
then for any
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
,
{\mathbf{t}}\in[1,\infty)^{2}
, and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{2}

(6.27)

\|L^{\bm{\nu}}g\|_{q}\leq c{\mathbf{t}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}+(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}){\mathbf{d}}}\|g\|_{p}.

Recall that
{\mathbf{d}}=(d_{1},d_{2})
,
{\bm{1}}:=(1,1)
, and
for the definition of
V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}
,
{\bm{\gamma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, see
(
6.4
)
.

Proof.

(i)
Let
g\in\Sigma_{\mathbf{t}}
,
{\mathbf{t}}\in[1,\infty)^{2}
, and set
{\bm{\delta}}:={\mathbf{t}}^{-1}=(t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}^{-1})
.
Let
\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\theta(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\theta(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})

and
\theta({\bm{\lambda}})=1
for
{\bm{\lambda}}\in[0,1]^{2}
.
Then
g=\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g
.
Let
\omega(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=\lambda_{1}^{2\nu_{1}}\lambda_{2}^{2\nu_{2}}\theta(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
.
Then

\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})={\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}L^{{\bm{\nu}}}\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})

and hence

(6.28)

L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{x}})=L^{\bm{\nu}}\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{x}})={\bm{\delta}}^{-2{\bm{\nu}}}\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{x}})={\bm{\delta}}^{-2{\bm{\nu}}}\int_{X}{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})g({\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

We use Corollary
4.22
and (
3.14
) to conclude that
for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in(0,\infty)^{2}

(6.29)

|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|\leq c_{\bm{\sigma}}V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\sigma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}).

We next show that inequality (
6.26
) is valid when
q=\infty
, i.e.

(6.30)

\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}L^{{\bm{\nu}}}g\big\|_{\infty}\leq c{\mathbf{t}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g\big\|_{p}.

Case 1:

1<p<\infty
.
We use (
6.28
) and Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}|L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|V(\cdot,\delta)^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g\big\|_{p}\Big(\int_{X}\big(|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-{\bm{\gamma}}+\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}\big)^{p^{\prime}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/p^{\prime}}

(6.31)

\displaystyle=:\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g\big\|_{p}\mathcal{Q}.

By (
3.17
) we have

(6.32)

V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-{\bm{\gamma}}+\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}\leq cV({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-{\bm{\gamma}}+\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}\prod_{i=1,2}\big(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\big)^{d_{i}|-\gamma_{i}+\frac{1}{p}|}.

Choose
\sigma_{i}:=d_{i}|-\gamma_{i}+\frac{1}{p}|+(d_{i}+1)/p^{\prime}

and set

\tilde{\sigma}_{i}:=p^{\prime}(\sigma_{i}-d_{i}|-\gamma_{i}+\frac{1}{p}|)
,
i=1,2
.
Clearly
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}=d_{i}+1
.
Using (
6.29
), (
6.32
), and (
3.19
) we obtain

\mathcal{Q}\leq V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-{\bm{\gamma}}-(1-\frac{1}{p}){\bm{1}}}\Big(\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\bm{\delta}},\tilde{{\bm{\sigma}}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\Big)^{1/p^{\prime}}\leq cV({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{-{\bm{\gamma}}}.

This coupled with (
6.31
) implies (
6.30
).

Case 2:

p=1
.
The proof of inequality (
6.30
) in this case is similar to the above proof but easier;
we omit it.

Case 3:

0<p<1
.
We first show that inequality (
6.30
) holds in this case when
{\bm{\nu}}=(0,0)
, i.e.

(6.33)

\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g\big\|_{\infty}\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\mathbf{t}}^{-1})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g\big\|_{p}.

To this end we first prove that this inequality is valid
with a constant
c=c(g)
depending on
g
.
The function
\theta({\bm{\lambda}})
from above obeys the assumptions of Proposition
5.7

and hence there exist constants
N,c>0
, depending on
g
, such that

(6.34)

\displaystyle|g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle=|\theta({\bm{\delta}}\sqrt{L})g({\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq c(g)V({\mathbf{x}}_{0},\delta)^{-1}\prod_{i=1,2}(\delta_{i}^{N}+\delta_{i}^{-N})\big(1+\rho_{i}(x_{i},x_{0i})\big)^{N}.

Now, precisely as in the proof of Theorem
6.4
(cf. (
6.17
))
with
r
replaced by
p
we obtain

\Big(\sup_{{\mathbf{z}}\in X}\frac{V({\mathbf{z}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{z}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i}))^{\tilde{N}}}\Big)^{p}\\
\leq c\int_{X}\frac{[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{p}}{V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})\prod_{i=1,2}(1+\delta_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\tilde{N}p}}d\mu({\mathbf{y}})\\
\leq c\int_{X}[V({\mathbf{y}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-(1/p){\bm{1}}}|g({\mathbf{y}})|]^{p}d\mu({\mathbf{y}}).

With
{\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{x}}
the above implies

(6.35)

\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}g(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq c(g)\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-(1/p){\bm{1}}}g(\cdot)\|_{p}.

Here the constant depends on
g
because
N
depends on
g
.

We are now ready to complete the proof of (
6.33
).
Applying (
6.30
) with
p=2
and
{\bm{\nu}}=(0,0)
we get

\displaystyle\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-(1/2){\bm{1}}}g\big\|_{2}

(6.36)

\displaystyle=c\Big(\int_{X}\big(V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|g({\mathbf{x}})|\big)^{2-p}\big(V({\mathbf{x}},{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-(1/p){\bm{1}}}|g({\mathbf{x}})|\big)^{p}d\mu({\mathbf{x}})\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}^{1-p/2}\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-(1/p){\bm{1}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p}^{p/2}.

If
\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g(\cdot)\|_{p}=\infty
, then there is nothing to prove.
If
\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g(\cdot)\|_{p}<\infty
,
then by (
6.35
)
\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{\bm{\gamma}}g(\cdot)\|_{\infty}<\infty

and we can divide in (
6.2
) by

\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g\big\|_{\infty}^{1-p/2}

to obtain (
6.33
).

Finally, using (
6.30
) with
p=2
, (
6.2
), and (
6.33
) we get

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}L^{{\bm{\nu}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{2}}g\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}^{1-p/2}\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p}^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}-\frac{{\bm{1}}}{p}}g\big\|_{p},

which completes the proof of (
6.30
).

We next prove (
6.26
) in the case
0<p<q<\infty
.
From Theorem
6.4
it follows that for any
{\bm{\gamma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}V({\mathbf{x}},\delta)^{{\bm{\gamma}}}|L^{\bm{\nu}}g({\mathbf{x}})|\leq c\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)({\mathbf{x}}).

We choose
0<r<p
and apply the maximal inequality (
4.104
) to obtain

(6.37)

\delta^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}L^{\bm{\nu}}g(\cdot)\|_{p}\leq c\|\mathcal{M}_{r}\big(V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\big)\|_{p}\leq c\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}g(\cdot)\|_{p}.

Finally, using (
6.30
) with
{\bm{\gamma}}
replaced by
{\bm{\gamma}}+\frac{{\bm{1}}}{q}

and (
6.37
) with
{\bm{\gamma}}
replaced by
{\bm{\gamma}}+(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}){\bm{1}}
we get

\displaystyle{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}

\displaystyle\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}}L^{\bm{\nu}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\delta^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}+\frac{{\bm{1}}}{q}}L^{\bm{\nu}}g\big\|_{\infty}\big)^{1-\frac{p}{q}}\big({\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}+(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}){\bm{1}}}L^{\bm{\nu}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p}\big)^{\frac{p}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{{\bm{\gamma}}+(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}){\bm{1}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p},

which confirms (
6.26
).

(ii) From (i) with
{\bm{\gamma}}=(0,0)
we derive

{\bm{\delta}}^{2{\bm{\nu}}}\|L^{\bm{\nu}}g\|_{q}\leq c\big\|V(\cdot,{\bm{\delta}})^{(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}){\bm{1}}}g(\cdot)\big\|_{p}

and using (
3.18
), which is a consequence of the non-collapsing condition (
2.6
),
we arrive at (
6.27
).
∎

7.
Product Hardy spaces

Hardy spaces
H^{p}
,
0<p\leq 1
, play a fundamental role in Harmonic analysis, see
[
19
]
and
[
47
]
and the references therein.
R. Coifman and G. Weiss
[
11
]
introduced and pioneered the theory of Hardy spaces on spaces of homogeneous type.
Later Hardy spaces have been extensively studied on Euclidean
[
18
]
and metric spaces associated with operators
[
35
]
.
Recently, in
[
15
]
the equivalence of Hardy spaces defined by several maximal operators as well as atomic Hardy spaces were obtained in
the setting of a doubling metric measure space
(X,\rho,\mu)
in the presence of a non-negative self-adjoint operator
L

whose heat kernel has Gaussian localization.
In this setting the Hardy space
H^{p}
,
0<p\leq 1
, is defined as the set of distributions
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
such that

(7.1)

\|f\|_{H^{p}}:=\big\|\sup_{t>0}|e^{-t^{2}L}f(\cdot)|\big\|_{p}<\infty.

Hardy spaces
H^{p}
,
0<p<\infty
, in the two-parameter classical setting have been developed mainly by
Sun-Yung Chang and R. Fefferman, see
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
20
]
and the references therein.
In the anisotropic setting the spaces have been studied in
[
4
,
5
]
.
Hardy spaces
H^{p}
,
p\geq 1
, have been introduced via area functions on product of spaces of homogeneous type associated with operators
[
9
]
.

In the present section we initiate the development of Hardy spaces
H^{p}
,
0<p<\infty
, in the two-parameter setting on product domains of this article.
We will introduce the bi-variable version of (
7.1
) and
establish equivalent characterizations via several maximal operators.

We will operate in the setting of this article (see Section
2
), assuming in addition that

\mu_{i}(X_{i})=\infty
,
i=1,2
. We start with the following

Definition 7.1
.

The product Hardy space
H^{p}=H^{p}(L_{1},L_{2})
,
0<p\leq 1
, is defined as the set of all
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(L_{1},L_{2})
such that

(7.2)

\|f\|_{H^{p}}:=\big\|\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}|e^{-t_{1}^{2}L_{1}-t_{2}^{2}L_{2}}f(\cdot)|\big\|_{p}<\infty.

The bi-variate nature of these spaces is exhibited in the vector-dilation of the parameter
{\mathbf{t}}=(t_{1},t_{2})
.

7.1.
Maximal operators and the main result

We next introduce several maximal operators that will be used in characterizing the two-parameter (product) Hardy spaces
H^{p}
.
We first introduce the class of admissible functions on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, suitable for the theory in the product setting.

Definition 7.2
.

We say that a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
is admissible if
\varphi
is real-valued,

\varphi(0,0)\neq 0
, and

(7.3)

\varphi(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\varphi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}),\quad\forall(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

Definition 7.3
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be an admissible function.
We define the following maximal operators:
Assuming
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbf{x}}\in X
, we set

(7.4)

M(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{x}})|,

(7.5)

M^{*}_{\mathbf{a}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}\sup_{\{{\mathbf{y}}\in X:\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq a_{i}t_{i}\}}|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|,\quad{\mathbf{a}}\geq{\bm{0}},

and

(7.6)

M^{**}_{\bm{\gamma}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}\frac{|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|}{\prod_{i=1,2}(1+t_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{\gamma_{i}}},\quad{\bm{\gamma}}>{\bm{0}}.

Using the compact notation
{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{t}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}):=\prod_{i=1,2}(1+t_{i}^{-1}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))^{-\gamma_{i}}
, see (
3.10
),
this can be written in the form

(7.7)

M^{**}_{\bm{\gamma}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}\sup_{{\mathbf{y}}\in X}|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{t}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}).

Obviously,

(7.8)

M(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})\leq M^{*}_{\mathbf{a}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})\leq({\bm{1}}+{\mathbf{a}})^{\bm{\gamma}}M^{**}_{\bm{\gamma}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}),\quad\forall f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\;{\mathbf{x}}\in X.

We next introduce the grand maximal operator.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be admissible and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We define

(7.9)

\mathcal{N}_{N}(\varphi):=\sup_{{\bm{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{N}\max_{0\leq|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N}|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})|

and set

\mathcal{F}_{N}:=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}):\varphi\;{\rm is\;admissible\;and}\;\;\mathcal{N}_{N}(\varphi)\leq 1\}.

The grand maximal operator is defined by

(7.10)

\mathscr{M}_{N}(f)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{F}_{N}}M^{*}_{\bm{1}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}}),\quad f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\;{\mathbf{x}}\in X,

that is,

(7.11)

\mathscr{M}_{N}(f)({\mathbf{x}}):=\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{F}_{N}}\sup_{{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}}\sup_{\{{\mathbf{y}}\in X:\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq t_{i}\}}|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|.

Observe that for any admissible function
\varphi
,
{\mathbf{a}}\geq{\bm{1}}
, and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(7.12)

M^{*}_{\mathbf{a}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})\leq|{\mathbf{a}}|^{N}\mathcal{N}_{N}(\varphi)\mathscr{M}_{N}(f)(x),\quad\forall f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\;{\mathbf{x}}\in X.

Indeed, consider
\phi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=A^{-1}\varphi(a_{1}^{-1}\lambda_{1},a_{2}^{-1}\lambda_{2})
, where
A:=|{\mathbf{a}}|^{N}\mathcal{N}_{N}(\varphi)
.
It is readily seen that

M^{*}_{\mathbf{a}}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})=AM^{*}_{\bm{1}}(f;\phi)({\mathbf{x}})

and a simple manipulation shows that

\mathcal{N}_{N}(\phi)\leq 1
.
Then (
7.12
) follows.

The main result in the section is the following

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
0<p\leq 1
.
Assume
N>6|{\mathbf{d}}|/p+3|{\mathbf{d}}|/2+3
,
{\mathbf{a}}\geq{\bm{1}}
,
{\bm{\gamma}}>(2/p){\mathbf{d}}

and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be admissible. Then for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

(7.13)

\|f\|_{H^{p}}\sim\|\mathscr{M}_{N}(f)\|_{L^{p}}\sim\|M(f;\varphi)\|_{L^{p}}\sim\|M^{*}_{\mathbf{a}}(f;\varphi)\|_{L^{p}}\sim\|M^{**}_{\bm{\gamma}}(f;\varphi)\|_{L^{p}}.

Here the equivalence constants depend on
{\mathbf{a}}
,
{\bm{\gamma}}
,
N
, and
\varphi
, respectively.

For the proof of this theorem we need some preparation.
We first establish an useful decomposition identity.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be admissible with
\varphi(0,0)=1
and let
N\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.
Then there exist an admissible function
\psi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
with
\psi(0,0)=1

and a function
\Psi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
with the properties

\Psi(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\Psi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
for
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and

\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\Psi(0,0)=0
for
|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N

such that for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

(7.14)

f=\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})f+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})\big(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L})\big)f,

where the convergence is in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbf{k}}:=(k,k)
,
{\bm{1}}:=(1,1)
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi
be from the hypothesis of the lemma and assume
N\geq 2
.
From the binomial formula it readily follows that

(7.15)

\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2m}(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}=1-(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N}

and for
k\geq 1

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}

\displaystyle(\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2}-\varphi(2^{-k+1}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{m}(1-\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}

\displaystyle=(1-\varphi(2^{-k+1}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N}-(1-\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N}.

Summing up the above identities and using that
\varphi(0,0)=1
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}

\displaystyle(\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2}-\varphi(2^{-k+1}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{m}(1-\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}=(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N}.

Combining this with (
7.15
) we find

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2m}(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}(\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2}-\varphi(2^{-k+1}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{m}(1-\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m},

which takes the form

(7.16)

1=\psi({\bm{\lambda}})\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Psi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})(\varphi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})-\varphi(2^{-k+1}{\bm{\lambda}})),

where

\psi({\bm{\lambda}}):=\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2m-1}(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}

and

\Psi({\bm{\lambda}}):=(\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})+\varphi(2{\bm{\lambda}}))\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\textstyle\binom{N}{m}}(\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2}-\varphi(2{\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{m-1}(1-\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})^{2})^{N-m}.

Note that since
\varphi
is admissible, then
\psi
and
\Psi
are admissible as well.
Also by the construction
\psi(0,0)=1
and
\partial^{\beta}\Psi(0,0)=0
for
|\beta|\leq N-2
.
We now replace
{\bm{\lambda}}
by
2{\bm{\lambda}}
in (
7.16
) and subtract the resulting identity from (
7.16
) to obtain

\Psi({\bm{\lambda}})(\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})-\varphi(2{\bm{\lambda}}))=\psi({\bm{\lambda}})\varphi({\bm{\lambda}})-\psi(2{\bm{\lambda}})\varphi(2{\bm{\lambda}}).

From this it readily follows that for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}{\bm{\lambda}})

\displaystyle(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}{\bm{\lambda}})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+1}{\bm{\lambda}}))

\displaystyle=\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}{\bm{\lambda}})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}{\bm{\lambda}})-\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}{\bm{\lambda}})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}{\bm{\lambda}}),\quad{\mathbf{m}}:=(m,m).

and hence for any
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})f

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-k}\sqrt{L})\big(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-k}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-k+1}\sqrt{L})\big)f

(7.17)

\displaystyle=\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}\sqrt{L})f.

From Theorem
5.10
it follows that

\lim_{m\to\infty}\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{m}}}\sqrt{L})f=f
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}

and passing to the limit in (
7.1
) as
m\to\infty
we obtain (
7.14
).
Finally, the lemma follows from the above considerations by replacing
N
with
N+2
.
∎

Our next step is to connect different maximal operators from Section
7.1
by means of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be admissible and
0<\theta\leq 1
. Let also
{\bm{\gamma}}=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})>{\bm{0}}
be so that
{\bm{\gamma}}>(2/\theta){\mathbf{d}}
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that

(7.18)

M_{{\bm{\gamma}}}^{**}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})\leq c\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(M(f;\varphi))({\mathbf{x}}),\quad\text{for}\;f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\;{\mathbf{x}}\in X,

where
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator, see (
4.102
).

Proof.

Let
\varphi,\theta
and
{\bm{\gamma}}
be from the hypothesis of the proposition.
Without loss of generality we may assume that
\varphi(0,0)=1
; otherwise we use
\varphi(0,0)^{-1}\varphi
instead.
Fix
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbf{x}}\in X
.

Part 1.

We first show that inequality (
7.18
) is valid under the additional assumption

(7.19)

M_{{\bm{\gamma}}}^{**}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})<\infty.

By Lemma
7.6
for any
N\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
there exists an admissible function
\psi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
with
\psi(0,0)=1

and a function
\Psi\in\mathcal{S}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
with the properties

\Psi(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\Psi(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
for
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
and
\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\Psi(0,0)=0
for
|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N

such that

f=\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})f+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L}))f,

where
{\mathbf{k}}:=(k,k)
.
We choose
N:=\lfloor 3|{\bm{\gamma}}|+3|{\mathbf{d}}|/2+4\rfloor
.

Fix
{\mathbf{t}}>{\bm{0}}
and let
{\mathbf{j}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
be such that
2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\leq{\mathbf{t}}<2^{-{\mathbf{j}}+{\bm{1}}}
.
In light of (
7.7
) we need to deal with
|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{t}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})
.
From above, using that
t_{i}\sim 2^{-j_{i}}
, we get

(7.20)

\displaystyle|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{t}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\leq c|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle+c\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})\big[\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L})\big]f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}).

Let
\omega({\bm{\lambda}}):=\varphi({\mathbf{t}}2^{{\mathbf{j}}}{\bm{\lambda}})\Psi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})
.
Then
\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})=\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})
.
Choose
m:=\lfloor\gamma_{1}+d_{1}/2+1\rfloor+\lfloor\gamma_{2}+d_{2}/2+1\rfloor
and
r:=m+|{\mathbf{d}}|+1
.
Since
\varphi
and
\Psi
are admissible, then
\omega\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\omega(\pm\lambda_{1},\pm\lambda_{2})=\omega(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
.
Also, using that
{\mathbf{t}}\sim 2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}
and
k\geq 1
we get

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\omega({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-N},\quad{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

From this it readily follow that

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\omega({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c2^{-k(N-r)/2}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-r},\quad\hbox{if $\;|{\bm{\lambda}}|\geq 2^{k/2}$, $|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N$,}

and since
N\geq 3|{\bm{\gamma}}|+3|{\mathbf{d}}|/2+3+2\tilde{{\varepsilon}}
for some
\tilde{{\varepsilon}}>0

it follows that

(7.21)

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\omega({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-r},\quad|{\bm{\lambda}}|\geq 2^{k/2},|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N.

On the other hand,
since
\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\Psi(0,0)=0
for
|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N
,
Taylor’s formula implies that

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\Psi({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c|{\bm{\lambda}}|^{N-|{\bm{\beta}}|}
for
|{\bm{\lambda}}|\leq 1
and
|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq N
.
Then for
|{\bm{\lambda}}|<2^{k/2}
, we get

\displaystyle|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}(\Psi(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}}))|=2^{-k|{\bm{\beta}}|}|(\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\Psi)(2^{-k}{\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c2^{-k|{\bm{\beta}}|}(2^{-k}|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{N-|{\bm{\beta}}|}\leq c2^{-kN/2}.

From this and the fact that
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
it follows that

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\omega({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c2^{-kN/2}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-N}\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-r},\quad|{\bm{\lambda}}|<2^{k/2}.

Combining this estimate with (
7.21
) leads to

(7.22)

|\partial^{{\bm{\beta}}}\omega({\bm{\lambda}})|\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}(1+|{\bm{\lambda}}|)^{-r},\quad{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},|{\bm{\beta}}|\leq m.

We use this and apply Theorem
4.21
with the roles of
k_{1},k_{2}
played by
\lfloor\gamma_{i}+d_{i}/2+1\rfloor
,
i=1,2
,
to obtain

(7.23)

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{K}}_{\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|=|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})|

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}{\mathfrak{D}}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}+{\mathbf{d}}/2}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}V({\mathbf{x}},2^{-{\mathbf{j}}})^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}),

where for the last inequality we used (
3.13
).
In turn this estimate implies

\displaystyle\big|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})

\displaystyle\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L}))f({\mathbf{y}})\big|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

(7.24)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\int_{X}|{\mathscr{K}}_{\omega(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\;\;\times(|\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{z}})|+|\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{z}})|)d\mu({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\int_{X}\frac{{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})}{V({\mathbf{z}},2^{-{\mathbf{j}}})}(|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k}({\mathbf{z}})|+|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k-1}({\mathbf{z}})|)d\mu({\mathbf{z}}),

where we used the short-hand notation
F_{{\mathbf{j}},k}({\mathbf{z}}):=\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{z}})
.

On the other hand,

1+2^{j_{i}}\rho_{i}(x_{i},z_{i})\leq(1+2^{j_{i}}\rho_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))(1+2^{j_{i}}\rho_{i}(y_{i},z_{i}))
and hence

2^{-k|{\bm{\gamma}}|}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}){\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})\leq 2^{-k|{\bm{\gamma}}|}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})\leq{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}}).

With
0<\theta\leq 1
from the hypothesis of the proposition this leads to

\displaystyle 2^{-k(|{\bm{\gamma}}|+\tilde{{\varepsilon}})}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{z}})|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k}({\mathbf{z}})|

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-k\tilde{{\varepsilon}}}M_{{\bm{\gamma}}}^{**}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})^{1-\theta}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}},\theta{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k}({\mathbf{z}})|^{\theta}.

Combining this with (
7.1
) we get

\displaystyle|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\Psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})(\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}}\sqrt{L})-\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-{\mathbf{k}}+{\bm{1}}}\sqrt{L}))f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-k\tilde{{\varepsilon}}}M_{{\bm{\gamma}}}^{**}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})^{1-\theta}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}-k},\theta{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})\frac{|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k}({\mathbf{z}})|^{\theta}+|F_{{\mathbf{j}},k-1}({\mathbf{z}})|^{\theta}}{V({\mathbf{z}},2^{-{\mathbf{j}}})}d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

In the same way we obtain

\displaystyle|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})\psi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})

\displaystyle\varphi(2^{-{\mathbf{j}}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle\leq cM_{{\bm{\gamma}}}^{**}(f;\varphi)({\mathbf{x}})^{1-\theta}\int_{X}{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{2^{-{\mathbf{j}}},\theta{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{z}})\frac{|F_{{\mathbf{j}},0}({\mathbf{z}})|^{\theta}}{V({\mathbf{z}},2^{-{\mathbf{j}}})}d\mu({\mathbf{z}}).

Combining the above estimates with (
7.20
) we obtain

\displaystyle|\varphi({\mathbf{t}}\sqrt{L})f({\mathbf{y}})|

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{t}},{\bm{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})

(7.25)
